amend-reason: 2026-09-19 MERGED with lc-208 per the driving desk's queue item 1. Same refusal row, same write-set, opposite directions of one predicate; lc-208 is DROPPED into this entry. The entry now carries TWO red-first arms proven separately, because a mutation on the shared expression darkens both and proves neither.
amended-requirement: 2026-09-19 migration_ledger_nonzero IS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AT ONE SITE, and the two defects are opposite readings of a single predicate. OVER-FIRE: it tests that the ledger count is not zero, where the count is the ledger lines AFTER the run rather than the lines the run itself routed, so every repo that has ever recorded a decision (a compaction included) gets a FINDING from any --merge, and the merge still writes. Law 11 shape: a guard firing on legitimate work, training the reader to discount the red that will one day be real. UNDER-FIRE: the same check counts len(lparsed.lines), the readable and classifiable lines only, while ledger.parse splits its input THREE ways (lines, unreadable, problems) and its own docstring says so. An unclassifiable ledger line therefore renders as 'ledger lines: 0' and the run exits CLEAN. One is law 11, the other is law 1, and neither is fixable without the other because both read the same expression. MERGED 2026-09-19 from lc-150 (over-fire, found by the lc-148/lc-145 lane) and lc-208 (unreadable half).
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-19 TWO RED-FIRST ARMS, both owed, because a fix for either direction alone leaves the other silently wrong. ARM 1, the over-fire: the row counts the lines the RUN routed, not the ledger total, so a --merge into a repo whose ledger already carries unrelated lines answers CLEAN, and a --merge that genuinely routes nothing still answers FINDING. The false-fire arm must go from FINDING to CLEAN on the fix; the true-fire arm must NOT move. ARM 2, the unreadable half: the ledger count consumes all THREE of ledger.parse's answers, so an unreadable line is could-not-verify with its reason and is never folded into a zero that reads as clean. Its pair: one well-formed superseded: line gives FINDING migration_ledger_nonzero at exit 2; one unclassifiable line in the same position must NOT give 'ledger lines: 0' and CLEAN. THE ARMS ARE PROVEN SEPARATELY, because a mutation on the shared expression darkens both and therefore proves neither (the lc-30 lesson: each direction takes the same-parentage mutation narrowed to itself, leaving the other reading real input). prove-rows keeps a recorded arrangement per arm, and each mutation must red only its own arm. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the registry stays ONE SOURCE for its two consumers (law 3).
amended-evidence: 2026-09-19 ARM 1 PROVENANCE, from lc-150: lane opus-lc148-145 closing report part 2a, gap 2; the lane's own migrate arm asserts the allocation off the carrier and carries a comment naming why the run answers FINDING. Its part 3b records the consequence: the merge's other answers are unverified by that lane because this over-fire masks the run's exit code. ARM 2 PROVENANCE, from lc-208, and the marks are carried verbatim because they are not all the same grade of evidence. RELAYED from the lc-192 lane with its executed pair pasted, and NOT reproduced at the booking desk: control, one well-formed superseded: line gives 'ledger lines: 1' and FINDING at exit 2; probe, one unclassifiable decision: line in the same position gives 'ledger lines: 0' and CLEAN at exit 0, while ledger.parse on that same file returns lines 0, unreadable 1, problems []. The arm that must differ differed. RELAYED, and marked as that lane's completed argument rather than a desk measurement: migrate writes no ledger line anywhere (2492-2493 writes head_text() only when the file is absent, and dispose_source writes the LAWS file, not the ledger), so a nonzero count is ALWAYS pre-existing content and the row makes a claim about a DELTA while its predicate is an ABSOLUTE count. MEASURED at the booking desk: this repo's own LEDGER.md parses to a nonzero line count, so the over-fire is reachable here and not hypothetical. DERIVED: law 11's discount reflex and law 22's unfalsifiable predicate meet in one row, which fires on legitimate work AND can never be a true positive. MERGE NOTE 2026-09-19: the two entries named the same site (migration_ledger_nonzero) and carried IDENTICAL write-sets, verified at the artifact via item slots before merging rather than assumed from their prose; lc-208's own done-criterion had already anticipated the merge, requiring the second direction be settled in the same change or explicitly deferred to lc-150.

## lc-151
grade: READY
requirement: AN UNRESOLVABLE HOME IS COUNTED AS AN EMPTY ONE, so the growth alarm is structurally blind on exactly the kinds whose growth is OUTSIDE the repo. list_home builds `repo / home` for every declared home, so a home that is not repo-relative can never resolve — and the miss is not an error but a silent count of 0, which the growth check then reports as CLEAN with the words `the home holds nothing, so nothing has grown`. Measured at the desk on the live tree at db50df2: kind `the fire log` declares $XDG_STATE_HOME/lifecycle/fire.jsonl and reports count 0 / CLEAN, while the real file is 112 MB and 1,035,153 lines; kind `plugin cache versions` declares ~/.claude/plugins/cache/<marketplace>/<plugin> and reports count 0 / CLEAN while 9 cached plugin version directories exist. A REGISTRY-WIDE SWEEP of all 21 kinds found exactly these two non-repo-relative homes and both are blind; every repo-relative home resolves. So the enumeration is closed, with the two members as its own positive controls.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/retire.py,test/test_retire.py
done-criterion: list_home distinguishes THREE answers where it now gives two: resolved-and-counted, resolved-and-empty, and COULD NOT RESOLVE — the third never reports CLEAN and never contributes a count of 0. A home is resolved against its own root rather than the repo: environment variables expanded with their XDG defaults (the live env has XDG_STATE_HOME EMPTY, so bare expansion yields /lifecycle/... and is NOT sufficient — the default $HOME/.local/state is what makes it resolve), `~` expanded, and a home carrying <placeholder> segments read as a pattern or refused at declaration time. Proven by a PAIR on each member: the fire-log kind reports a NON-zero count on a machine where the log exists, and reports COULD NOT VERIFY — not CLEAN — when the path is absent. The mutation that reds the arm must be an assertion FAILURE, read off the failures=/errors= split.
evidence: Desk integration verification of wave 3 at db50df2, live tree, read-only audit run. Contradiction inside ONE report is what surfaced it: the audit header reads `exit events read from the fire log: 2 record(s) for this repo` — so read_fire_log resolves the path — while the fire-log KIND in the same walk reports its home `is not present`. read_fire_log is the existing instance the repair reuses; list_home is the site that does not.
blocked-by: NONE

## lc-153
grade: READY
requirement: THE REPO'S DOCUMENTED LEAK-SCAN ARMING IS INERT, and the scan is protecting this repo only through a second path the repo does not know it depends on. CLAUDE.md:208-213 states the scan `is armed as this repo's pre-push hook (tools/git-hooks/pre-push, symlinked into .git/hooks/) from the repo's first commit, because this repo is where workflow templates extracted from PRIVATE repos will land. No template is extracted until that hook exists.` The symlink EXISTS exactly as documented (.git/hooks/pre-push -> ../../tools/git-hooks/pre-push, dated 26 Aug) AND GIT NEVER CONSULTS IT: core.hooksPath is set, both locally and globally, to the DOTFILES repo's own git/hooks directory, and core.hooksPath OVERRIDES .git/hooks entirely. Measured with a positive control rather than by reasoning: `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` — git's own answer, not the raw config string — resolves to that foreign directory (and note git EXPANDS a leading tilde there itself, so a tilde in the config value is not evidence of non-resolution); and the attribution text `WITHOUT A MARK`, observed in this desk's real pushes, occurs 7 times in the dotfiles hook and 0 times in this repo's, so the hook that ran was definitively the foreign one. Nothing has leaked and the scan does run — it runs because ANOTHER REPO's hook invokes it (23 references there), not because of the mechanism this repo documents and relies on.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: CLAUDE.md,tools/git-hooks/pre-push
done-criterion: The repo's own statement about its arming is TRUE of the machine it runs on, and the guarantee does not rest on an unstated dependency. Either (a) the repo states that core.hooksPath, when set, is what executes, names the foreign hook as the actual runner, and says what happens if that config is unset or repointed; or (b) the repo makes its own hook reachable regardless. EITHER WAY the claim `no template is extracted until that hook exists` is replaced by one about the hook that EXECUTES, since existence was never the property that mattered. PROVEN BY A PAIR, not by reading: an arm shows the scan running when the documented condition holds, and a second shows what happens with core.hooksPath unset — the second is the one that reveals whether the protection survives at all. A check asserting only that the symlink exists is the exact defect being repaired and may not be the verifier.
evidence: Found by opus-lc120 while measuring whether committing part of its work would leave main red. It read tools/git-hooks/pre-push and correctly concluded no battery runs at pre-push, but attributed that to an unexpanded tilde making hooks not run at all. The desk re-measured: git expands the tilde itself, the hooks DO run, and the file the lane read is the one git BYPASSES. Right conclusion, wrong mechanism — and the wrong mechanism was the dangerous half, since `no pre-push runs here` would license assuming there is no leak gate, when the leak gate is precisely what does run and blocked this desk's own push twice today, the second time over this very item's first draft.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-15 The desk settling an OPEN DESIGN CHOICE before dispatch rather than letting a lane pick it. The entry as first booked offered (a) document the real mechanism or (b) make the repo's own hook reachable — two different guarantees, and a lane choosing between them would be making a design decision the booking should have made. (b) is now ruled OUT on a fact rather than a preference: git consults exactly ONE hooks directory, so no change inside this repo can make its own hook run while core.hooksPath points elsewhere, and shipping (b) would mean shipping a guarantee the machine does not honour. The cross-repo residue (a delegating foreign hook) is named in the criterion and surfaced to the judgment desk, so it is not discharged by being mentioned here.
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-15 RULED (a), so no lane picks it: the repo STOPS claiming a mechanism it does not have and names the one it actually depends on. CLAUDE.md:208-213 is rewritten to say that where core.hooksPath is set it is the ONLY hooks directory git consults, that .git/hooks and therefore this repo's own tools/git-hooks/pre-push are bypassed on such a machine, that the leak scan currently runs because the configured foreign hook invokes it, and what is true if that config is unset or repointed. The sentence 'no template is extracted until that hook exists' is replaced by one about the hook that EXECUTES, because existence was never the property that mattered. PROVEN BY A PAIR, not by reading: an arm shows the scan running under the configured hooksPath, and a second shows the answer with core.hooksPath unset — the second is the one that reveals whether any protection survives. A check asserting only that the symlink exists is the exact defect being repaired and may not be the verifier. NOT IN SCOPE and deliberately not attempted: making this repo's own hook reachable regardless. Git consults exactly one hooks directory, so a repo cannot force its own hook from the inside; the durable fix is for the FOREIGN hook to delegate to a repo-local one, which is another repo's write boundary and another desk's call. The item records that dependency as a standing fact rather than leaving a later reader to rediscover it.
amend-reason: 2026-09-15 The cross-repo residue now has a decided home, so the text stops hedging. When this criterion was first ruled, the delegating-hook fix was a possibility I named without a venue — an open end, which is the shape that never gets closed because no carrier holds it. The judgment desk booked it as dotfiles df-254 with the design settled at booking, so the honest sentence changed: not 'someone could decide this' but 'this is decided and lands there'. Recorded as an amendment rather than by rewriting the earlier line, because the earlier line was TRUE when written and the sequence is the evidence that the residue was named before it was booked, not after.
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-15 RULED (a), so no lane picks it: the repo STOPS claiming a mechanism it does not have and names the one it actually depends on. CLAUDE.md:208-213 is rewritten to say that where core.hooksPath is set it is the ONLY hooks directory git consults, that .git/hooks and therefore this repo's own tools/git-hooks/pre-push are bypassed on such a machine, that the leak scan currently runs because the configured foreign hook invokes it, and what is true if that config is unset or repointed. The sentence 'no template is extracted until that hook exists' is replaced by one about the hook that EXECUTES, because existence was never the property that mattered. PROVEN BY A PAIR, not by reading: an arm shows the scan running under the configured hooksPath, and a second shows the answer with core.hooksPath unset — the second is the one that reveals whether any protection survives. A check asserting only that the symlink exists is the exact defect being repaired and may not be the verifier. NOT IN SCOPE here and NOT left open either: git consults exactly one hooks directory, so no change inside this repo can make its own hook run while core.hooksPath points elsewhere. THE DECIDING VENUE EXISTS AND IS NAMED — the dotfiles repo's item df-254, booked and pushed at 503cd42, which gives the machine-wide hook DELEGATION: after the global gates pass it executes a repo's own .git/hooks/<name>, propagating its exit, with absence unchanged. So this repo's text says its protection currently depends on the configured foreign hook AND points at df-254 as where that dependency is being removed, rather than trailing off in 'until someone decides otherwise'.

## lc-154
grade: READY
requirement: LAW 24's `must move with` HALF HAS NO OBSERVER AT THE MOMENT IT FIRES. The law says a write boundary is complete only once every change resolves to what REALIZES it and to every file that must MOVE WITH it, and the second half is checked today by a human noticing. For ONE measurable shape it is computable: where the change edits a SOURCE-DERIVABLE ENUMERATION, the files that derive that enumeration and grade themselves against it are greppable BEFORE a lane is briefed. Measured instance: lc-120 added a member to cli.py's `item_action` tuple; test/test_verbs.py derives that tuple from source and compares it to a hand-written DESTINATIONS table, so it went red although it realizes nothing. The cost of having no observer was one lane halt, one round trip to the desk, and one grant — with the work already built and green.
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: tools/write-set-dependents.py,test/test_tools.py
done-criterion: Given the location of a source-derivable enumeration, a tool lists the tracked files that DERIVE it and assert against it, for inclusion in a write set at booking time. PROVEN ON A PAIR drawn from the repo, never on a planted row: the KNOWN POSITIVE is cli.py's `item_action` tuple, whose answer MUST contain test/test_verbs.py — the file whose absence cost lc-120 its halt; the KNOWN NEGATIVE is an enumeration no file grades, whose answer must be empty. A tool that returns test_verbs.py for every input has not discriminated. ITS OUTPUT SET HAS THREE ANSWERS, not two: an enumeration it cannot resolve answers COULD NOT VERIFY and names what it could not read, never an empty list, because an empty list here reads as `nothing depends on this` and is the exact false clean the item exists to prevent.
evidence: Ruled a repo booking by the judgment desk on the wave-3 law-24 round, after lc-120's lane surfaced the shape and explicitly declined to generalise it: one instance, one measurement, a hypothesis rather than a class. That grade is carried here deliberately — the tool is scoped to the source-derivable-enumeration shape and claims nothing beyond it.
blocked-by: NONE

## lc-155
grade: READY
requirement: Carrier citations rot silently in THREE kinds, each needing its own cure — measured 2026-09-17 in CachyOS-Setup ITEMS.md by the freeze-arc wave close (peer desk cachyos-setup-02, all instances verified at that repo's base 43305a3): (1) WRONG POINTER — a cited line or named artifact does not hold the claim (4 instances: a :162 for content at 435; a :42 and a :101 for content at 366-370; a named prototype script existing nowhere on disk); (2) WRONG FIGURE — a stated count diverging from the primary record ("~180 freezes" where the session's own log says 558); (3) CORRECT POINTER, STALE CONTENT — the citation resolves but the text it names has gone stale (a known-issues line naming 610.43.03 as current on a 615.71.09 box; misled a human reader for a month). Rate with its denominator: 3 of 7 explicit line/count citations wrong across one lane's five entries, concentrated in the file that changes most. A resolver-only checker catches kind 1 alone; kind 3 is invisible to it; kind 2 needs the figure re-derived from the primary record.
goal: general-maintenance
write-set: lifecycle plugin checker code beside the existing shape/conservation checks; the compose-time convention text in the plugin's docs
done-criterion: a lifecycle check resolves each entry's cited file:line and named artifacts against the working tree and reports THREE answers per citation (resolves-and-matches / resolves-but-diverged / gone) — "matches" checkable only for citations carrying a content anchor (a quoted fragment beside the pointer), entries without anchors reported as UNANCHORED rather than passed; red-first on fixtures reconstructed from the measured cs-36/cs-37/cs-39 instances; the companion convention (a citation carries a quoted fragment beside its line number — the dispatch-brief content-anchor rule generalized to carrier entries) stated in the plugin docs
evidence: CachyOS-Setup freeze-arc wave close 2026-09-17, desk session 04c231 digest; peer desk measurement message with per-instance bases at 43305a3; dispatch skill section 1 content-anchor clause (the same rule at brief grain)
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 2026-09-18 write-set repaired to PATHS ONLY. The slot carried a real path with a parenthetical glued to it, which does not parse as a path and made item waves exit COULD NOT VERIFY for the whole carrier. The doctrine demote-to-parked clause is for a boundary genuinely UNKNOWN; this was the inverse, a known path made unparseable by commentary. The commentary is not lost: it restates what the requirement already says. Found by the build desk, ruled by this desk.
amended-write-set: 2026-09-18 plugin/cli/lifecycle_core,test/

## lc-159
grade: READY
requirement: EVERY CARRIER WRITE IS NON-ATOMIC AND A TRUNCATED CARRIER PARSES CLEAN — the tool's own worst instance of the class lc-157 is about. 16 carrier write sites (items_path/done_path/ledger_path) use Path.write_text, which truncates then writes; no atomic helper exists in the package (the sole os.replace is a docstring at declaration.py:1290). An interrupt between truncate and flush leaves the carrier SHORT, and the parse contract keys on the head, so the short file returns refused=False with FEWER items and NO problems. Every downstream consumer then gets a confident, clean, WRONG answer over a smaller carrier — the tool's deliberate third answer (unknown/refuse) is routed around entirely.

## lc-161
grade: READY
requirement: MEASURE DECISION ABSORPTION, NOT ONLY ERROR CATCHING. Operator, 2026-09-18, their own account of why they run desk/peer heavily: it lets them LEAVE. The peer poses decisions, the desk answers them, and the work continues without the operator, who has learned from experience roughly how far the desk's judgment can be trusted. That makes the primary measurable value DECISION ABSORPTION rather than error catching, and the catch study now running (lc-157 arc) measures a secondary effect. It also explains why no comparison has ever been available: the counterfactual to a desk/peer pair is not one session doing the same work, it is ONE SESSION THAT STALLS waiting for the operator. The corpus already carries the principle this operationalizes, the kind split, where work divides between desk and operator by kind and operator attention neither scales nor parallelizes; desk/peer is that split made structural.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN. A second extraction pass over the same 9-session sample the catch study used; the instrument is the same extractor, the classification is different
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending design, but the shape is known: per desk/peer session pair, count decisions the PEER POSED and classify each as ABSORBED (the desk answered it and work continued), ESCALATED (it reached the operator anyway), or STALLED (work waited). The ratio of absorbed to escalated is the decision-absorption rate, and unlike the catch study this needs no control arm to be meaningful, because a stall is observable in the transcript as elapsed time with no progress.
evidence: Operator, 2026-09-18, first-hand and in their own framing: they use desk/peer 'so i can leave the desk to make decisions that the peer poses so it runs on its own because i know roughly how far i can trust its judgement (from experience)'. Same sample is already characterized: 168 sessions carry peer traffic, 3203 peer messages and 6799 operator interjections machine-wide; the 9-session study sample each have at least 20 peer and 20 operator messages. TRUST CALIBRATION IS THE UNMEASURED HALF and is named here rather than folded in: the operator's confidence is built from experience across many sessions, is not transferable to a new desk, and nothing in the corpus or the tool records what it is calibrated ON. That is a separate question and probably a separate item.
blocked-by: decision should the absorption pass run over the same sample as the catch study or a fresh one?
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 2026-09-18 operator raised long-running single sessions as a counterexample (~/wan2gp), and it measured out: autonomy without a desk, apparently bought by persisted guidance instead. Folded in here rather than minted separately because it answers THIS item's question — what makes work continue without the operator — with a second mechanism, and splitting it would put two answers to one question in two carriers
amended-evidence: 2026-09-18 Operator, 2026-09-18, first-hand: they use desk/peer 'so i can leave the desk to make decisions that the peer poses so it runs on its own because i know roughly how far i can trust its judgement (from experience)'. SECOND ROUTE TO THE SAME OUTCOME, operator-raised and MEASURED at this desk the same day, and it is a counterexample to any reading that makes the pairing necessary: ~/wan2gp runs long autonomous SINGLE sessions. Operator interjections per 100 assistant turns, from the raw transcripts: 3339 turns / 135 interjections = 4.0; 1680 / 81 = 4.8; 684 / 6 = 0.9; 323 / 0 = 0.0 — with peer traffic at 0 to 43 messages, i.e. little or none. So hundreds to thousands of turns run with almost no operator input and no desk. The operator's own hypothesis for why, which the persisted artifacts support: that repo carries unusually rich persisted guidance — CLAUDE.md 553 lines, BACKLOG.md 608KB (2.7x lifecycle's entire item carrier), plus BEGEHUNG-MAP.md. READING: persisted guidance and a desk are two routes to the same property, autonomy, because both let the session answer a question WITHOUT the operator — one by querying a record, the other by asking a party. That is the research note's 'persistence guides by mere existence' claim, which the literature scout found NOBODY measures (gap 1 of 3, the named missing ablation being the arm where a record is present but never referenced). This is the first operational evidence for it on this machine and it arrived as a counterexample rather than as a confirmation, which is why it is worth more than a supporting datum. UNMEASURED and not to be assumed: whether wan2gp's sessions are as RELIABLE as they are autonomous — turn count and interjection rate say nothing about whether the output was right, and the catch study's instrument cannot see a single session's self-catches at all. TRUST CALIBRATION remains the unmeasured half of the desk route: the operator's confidence is built from experience across many sessions, is not transferable to a new desk, and nothing records what it is calibrated on.
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 DECIDED AT THIS DESK: the SAME 9-session sample as the catch study. It is already characterized (inbound counts per session, governance status, domain, operator-message volume), the extractor exists and is positive-controlled, and a fresh sample would buy a new characterization for no stated gain. The known limitation rides with it and is already recorded: governed and ungoverned repos in that sample share no domain coordinate, so the absorption figures will be indicative per session rather than a population parameter, and must be reported that way.

## lc-165
grade: READY
requirement: AN AMENDED ENTRY HEAD KEEPS THE SUPERSEDED VALUE AND A READER WHO STOPS EARLY GETS THE WRONG STORY. Amendments stack below the original, so the body a reader REACHES FIRST is the one no longer true. Measured in operation today by a peer desk: cs-35 took four amendments in about 90 minutes (requirement wrong, then evidence, then the blocker twice) and now carries superseded lines above the true state; cs-48 carries two amend-reason and two amended-evidence pairs while its LIVE requirement line still describes a design a later amendment says is not buildable. This is the label-over-body class inside one entry, and the dispatch discipline already names it from the outside: a re-grade written as an adjacent record while the original head keeps its live grade, measured twice, each time costing a lane.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: the carrier RENDERS the current effective value distinctly from its history, so the body a reader reaches first is the one that is TRUE. The item slots verb already resolves amendments and is the precedent; the gap is the RAW block, which is what a human and every non-tool reader actually opens. RED-FIRST: cs-48 shape (a live requirement contradicted by its own later amendment) is the firing input, and a reader of the rendered entry must reach the amended value before the superseded one. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the amendment HISTORY stays readable, because law 8 says both reads exist since neither is sufficient, and a rendering that hides what was superseded trades one wrong story for another.
evidence: Peer desk cachyos-setup-33, 2026-09-18, from eight amendments in operation: cs-35 four amendments in about 90 minutes; cs-48 with its live requirement contradicted by its own later amendment. THIS DESK OWN INSTANCES THE SAME DAY: lc-158, lc-159 and lc-161 were each amended after booking, and lc-159 amendment reversed the scope fivefold, its original done-criterion reading as a cross-repo schema migration that the amendment kills. Law 8 two-reads rule is both the precedent and the boundary.

## lc-173
grade: READY
requirement: refusals.py IS A STRUCTURAL SERIALIZER AND IT IS LAW 2 THAT MAKES IT ONE. Measured 2026-09-18 over the eight items then open: refusals.py appears in SIX of the eight write-sets, items.py in four, verbs.py and declaration.py in three each. The cause is not booking grain — it is law 2 itself, "every refusal is a registry row", so EVERY item that emits a finding must edit the one file. The consequence is that no amount of parallel dispatch buys elapsed time on finding-emitting work: worktree lanes would each add rows to refusals.py and collide at integration. The law that makes checks provable is the same law that makes the work serial, and that tradeoff is currently invisible — the join reports the collision without naming its cause, so each desk re-derives it.
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/roster.py
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the decision below. The shape of a fix, if one is wanted: rows live in per-area modules that the roster imports, so two items adding rows to different areas do not touch one file. The shape of NOT fixing it: the join names the cause when refusals.py is the shared member, so a desk reads "this is law 2 serializing you" rather than re-deriving it. MUST-NOT-MOVE either way: the registry stays ONE SOURCE for its two consumers (the acceptance test and --test), which is law 3, and a split that produced two rosters would trade a scheduling cost for a correctness one.
evidence: Measured at this desk 2026-09-18 while assessing whether the open set could be parallelized: refusals.py in 6 of 8 write-sets (lc-164, 167, 168, 169, 170, 172), items.py in 4, verbs.py and declaration.py in 3. Peer desk throughput on the same day, from commit timestamps: four items in about 85 minutes, roughly 21 minutes each, all serial. The peer independently routed the batch inline and serially and named the join as its basis, so two desks reached the same conclusion from the same artifact. Law 2 text: every refusal is a registry row with the input that fires it, proven red first.
blocked-by: decision is the serializer worth removing, or worth naming in the join output?

## lc-178
grade: READY
requirement: A DEMAND ADDED AT A WRITE DOOR CONTAMINATES EVERY ROSTER CONTROL THAT PASSES THROUGH THAT DOOR, AND NOTHING ENUMERATES THAT POPULATION. Measured three times in one day, by the same desk, each time discovered by RUNNING rather than by looking: lc-30's row retired a neighbour's proof; lc-167's mark demand broke 67 test arms, 35 of them from one shared plant; lc-169's derivability demand broke FOUR neighbours' roster controls at once (blocker_untyped, blocker_unstorable, parked_without_typed_blocker, new_without_typed_blocker) because every one of them proves its own row with a CLEAN control that books a decision blocker. A roster control is a fixture that must pass cleanly; adding a demand at the door it walks through makes it fail for a reason that has nothing to do with the row it proves. The cost is paid at run time, after the change is written, and it is paid again by every later item touching the same door — lc-165, lc-170 and lc-171 all touch verbs the controls use.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/roster.py,tools/prove-rows.py,test/test_roster.py
done-criterion: for a new refusal at door D, the rows whose CONTROL invokes D are listable BEFORE the demand is written. OUTCOME FIRST: a session adding a door demand can read the blast radius instead of discovering it from a red suite. The computable slice is exactly that listing — the roster already declares each row's firing input and control, so the population is derivable from the roster rather than restated beside it (law 17's restated-enumeration trap: a hardcoded list beside the source it mirrors cannot age loudly). RED-FIRST: with lc-169's derivability demand as the input, the listing must name those four rows and no others — the incident IS the fixture, and a listing that names all rows or none discriminates nothing. MUST-NOT-MOVE: this is a READOUT, never a refusal. A guard that blocked a door demand until its controls were updated would fire on legitimate work (law 11), and the whole point is that the contamination is legitimate and merely unseen.
evidence: MEASURED by the peer desk lifecycle-8c 2026-09-18, three instances in one day, and CARRIED AS THEIRS on the counts: lc-30 (a row addition retiring blocker_predicate_broken's proof, repaired in code not in the arrangement), lc-167 (67 arms, 35 from one shared plant), lc-169 (four named roster controls). THEIR OWN READING, adopted as this item's requirement because it is the generalisation and they reached it from the instances rather than from a rule: the roster's controls are a population nothing enumerates, and they were found by running rather than by looking. DERIVED AT THIS DESK: this is law 26's first clause pointed at a design-time question — what must be WRITTEN (here, listed) at the moment a door demand is added, whose absence is computable — and lc-30's own laws-file paragraph already describes the single-row case ('adding a row can retire a neighbour's proof, and only prove-rows says so'); what is new is that the population is not one neighbour but every control walking that door. LIVE RELEVANCE, not historical: lc-165, lc-170 and lc-171 are queued and all touch verbs the controls use, so the next three items are expected instances.

amend-reason: 2026-09-18 lc-171 is the fourth instance and the first where the red-first arm passed while reach was broken
amended-evidence: 2026-09-18 MEASURED by the peer desk lifecycle-8c 2026-09-18, now FOUR instances in one day and the fourth is the sharpest because it was that desk's OWN work rather than inherited: lc-30 (a row addition retiring blocker_predicate_broken's proof), lc-167 (67 arms, 35 from one shared plant), lc-169 (four named roster controls at one door), and lc-171 — a new declaration check that over-fired and put 22 ROSTER ROWS into COULD NOT VERIFY in a single run, because every declaration row runs kind check over a SCRATCH repo while the check read the machine's real desk-state home. THE INSIDE VIEW, carried verbatim as the requirement's own restatement and the reason this item is worth building: the withdrawn check was written, wired, and seen RED on the real repo before the over-fire was found — THE RED-FIRST ARM PASSED AND TOLD THEM NOTHING ABOUT REACH. What caught it was the roster's existing controls, not the new arm. So a new demand's red proves the CLASS that fired and never its reach, and the population that reveals reach is exactly the set of existing controls passing through the same door — which is this item's listing, stated from the inside by a desk that hit it. DERIVED: the prediction in this item's own evidence slot ('lc-165, lc-170 and lc-171 are queued and all touch verbs the controls use, so expect instances') FIRED on lc-171 within hours of being written, which is a registered prediction confirmed rather than a pattern noticed after the fact. THE REPAIR THAT DESK TOOK IS THE ONE THIS ITEM ENDORSES: the check and its row were WITHDRAWN together with the measurement recorded where the check was, rather than the predicate softened or the row's fixture scoped — either would have left a check that passes because nothing reaches it.
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 carrying the peer's own sentence verbatim: a pair proves the axis, the silent arm proves the reach
amended-requirement: 2026-09-18 A DEMAND ADDED AT A WRITE DOOR CONTAMINATES EVERY ROSTER CONTROL THAT PASSES THROUGH THAT DOOR, AND NOTHING ENUMERATES THAT POPULATION. THE SENTENCE THAT STATES THE MECHANISM, from the desk whose own proof failed on it and carried verbatim because it is sharper than any restatement: 'a row's pair proves the refusal's AXIS and never its REACH; reach is proven by the arm that must stay SILENT.' ## lc-211
grade: READY
requirement: THE --repo FLAG-ORDER ERROR TELLS A CALLER THEIR REPO PATH IS BAD WHEN THE FAULT IS ARGUMENT ORDER. 'lifecycle kind check --repo X' exits 3 on an argparse usage error. The exit code is RIGHT — could-not-verify, per the tool's own contract — and the MESSAGE is wrong: it reads as a bad repo path and sends the caller to check a path that is fine. A misleading true verdict costs more than a missing one, because it spends the reader's attention in the wrong place and they leave believing they checked something. argparse can see a --repo token sitting after the subcommand.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_refusals.py

The exit code stays 3 — the contract is already right and is not touched. RED-FIRST: 'lifecycle kind check --repo <a path that exists>' currently exits 3 with a path-shaped message; after the change the message names the order and the path is not implicated. CONTROL that must stay green: '--repo <bad path>' BEFORE the subcommand still reports a bad path, because that is the case where the path really is the fault — and that arm is what proves the new message discriminates rather than replacing one blanket wording with another.
evidence: RELAYED from the verb-io-surface audit (docs/audits/2026-09-18-verb-io-surface.md, commit 9600455, lower-ranked cells) and from lifecycle-6f, who REVERSED their own not-item-shaped call on it and asked for it to be booked — carried as their judgment, which I agree with. ## lc-212
grade: READY
requirement: AN EMPTY ROSTER EXITS CLEAN, BETWEEN TWO NEIGHBOURS THAT BOTH REFUSE, AND THE ABSENT-ROSTER GUARD'S OWN TEXT DESCRIBES THE HAZARD IT THEN PERMITS. lane list is the router — the one thing that says whether any declared repo has a lane needing attention — and it is GENERATED over the roster. An absent roster is a FINDING and an unresolvable entry is a FINDING, but a roster listing nothing prints 'lanes: 0 total FIRE 0 QUIET 0 BROKEN 0' and 'lane list: CLEAN' at exit 0. A caller scripting on that exit is told nothing needs attention by an instrument that was never pointed at anything. The states that produce an empty roster — a fresh machine, a lost config, a failed lane register — are exactly the ones where a green board is most misleading.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/lanes.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_lane_list_json.py
done-criterion: An empty roster is a FINDING with its own row, separate from roster_absent. FINDING and not could-not-verify, on the same argument the absent case already makes in its own words: a roster listing no repo is a STATE OF THE SYSTEM, not a limit of this run. SEPARATE ROW and not a widened roster_absent: an operator told only 'your board is empty' cannot tell a missing file from a file with nothing in it, and the repairs differ — create the roster versus lane register something. RED-FIRST, THREE ARMS, AND THE THIRD IS THE ONE THAT BOUNDS THE FIX: (1) absent roster still FINDING, unchanged; (2) empty roster goes from CLEAN to FINDING naming its own repair; (3) CONTROL THAT MUST STAY GREEN — a roster listing one repo that declares ZERO lanes still exits 0, because the laws file says such a repo 'says so in a line of its own', so an over-broad fix that refuses every zero-lane board breaks the documented good case.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk, three arms plus the control, XDG_CONFIG_HOME redirected to a scratch root and the real roster verified intact at 3 lines afterwards — that isolation is not incidental, it is the exact bite lifecycle-6f's own lane took when it wrote to the operator's real roster. ABSENT -> exit 2, 'lane list: FINDING — the router could not be generated. This is a FINDING and not a could-not-verify: §3.3 names an absent roster BROKEN, which is a state of the system rather than a limit of this run.' EMPTY -> exit 0, 'lanes: 0 total FIRE 0 QUIET 0 BROKEN 0', 'lane list: CLEAN'. ONE UNRESOLVABLE ENTRY -> exit 2, FINDING. CONTROL, one repo declaring zero lanes -> exit 0, 'roster count: 1 repo(s) listed'. MEASURED guard text at the artifact, read rather than relayed: lanes.py:341-345 refuses the absent case saying 'with no roster there is no board at all — and an empty board renders exactly like a board on which every lane is quiet', and lanes.py:531-533 writes a roster header saying 'an absent roster is BROKEN, never an empty board'. Read in context the distinction was drawn to justify refusing ABSENT; the EMPTY case was never asked, and the sentence describes it precisely. DERIVED AT THIS DESK and it sharpens the case: lane register writes the header and the first entry in the SAME operation (lanes.py:528-534, 'if not existed' then fh.write(resolved)), so the tool NEVER produces an empty roster — an empty one is always hand-made, emptied, or a partial write, which means it always signals something went wrong rather than an ordinary starting state. RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, who found it while answering my worst([]) question and whose three arms I reproduced rather than carried.

## lc-216
grade: READY
requirement: LAW 2 SAYS EVERY REFUSAL IS A REGISTRY ROW, AND THE CODE ADMITS 3 COULD-NOT-VERIFY REFUSALS OUT OF 196 EMISSIONS. THE QUESTION IS WHETHER THE LAW'S REACH IS NARROWER THAN ITS TEXT OR THE REGISTRY HAS A POPULATION IT NEVER ADMITTED — AND IT MUST NOT BE ANSWERED BY ADDING A ROW. This repo's whole discipline says could-not-verify is a FIRST-CLASS verdict, not a soft finding. Law 2 is written over refusals without saying which verdicts count. Today's ruling already moved the line once: roster.py:65-74 records that the emit-site scan matched 'FINDING [' alone, so a refusal emitted as COULD NOT VERIFY was invisible to it, and _LITERAL_CNV was added. Three sites took the bracket. 193 did not. Nothing decides which of the 193 are refusals in law 2's sense, and because the scanner keys on the BRACKET, an unbracketed could-not-verify is outside the registry's reach BY CONSTRUCTION — it cannot be counted as missing, so the coverage check's denominator has never included it. THE ANSWER DECIDES WHETHER THE ROSTER'S DENOMINATOR HAS BEEN WRONG ALL ALONG, which is why adding one row would bury the question rather than settle it.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: CLAUDE.md,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/roster.py
done-criterion: Law 2's reach over the third answer is DECIDED and written where the law is, in one of exactly two directions, each with a consequence that is then built. EITHER the law covers findings only and says so — in which case the three bracketed could-not-verify rows are the anomaly and the scanner's CNV matcher is documented as an opt-in channel rather than a coverage claim. OR could-not-verify refusals are registry-bearing — in which case the registry has 193 unadmitted emissions and the coverage check's assurance is WIDER THAN ITS PREDICATE, which is this repo's own label-over-body class at the instrument that grades every other one. MUST-NOT-BUILD, and it is the whole point: this is NOT resolved by adding a roster row for any site, mine included. A row would make one site green and leave the denominator question exactly where it was. RED-FIRST for whichever direction wins: the coverage check states in its own printed LIMIT which verdict channels it covers, and a planted emission in an UNCOVERED channel must NOT be reported as covered — an assertion on what must not come back, since the failure here is an assurance reading wider than it is.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk, executed, with a positive control: 'COULD NOT VERIFY' appears 196 times across the core package and 'COULD NOT VERIFY [' — the bracketed, registry-bearing form — exactly 3; the control 'FINDING [' returns 125, so the pattern discriminates rather than matching nothing. The unbracketed 193 are spread across verbs.py 39, cli.py 33, migrate.py 31, items.py 20, retire.py 18, refusals.py 13 and six more files. MEASURED mechanism: roster.py:74 defines _LITERAL_CNV to match the bracketed form, and its own comment at 65-66 records the ruling — 'This scan matched FINDING [ alone, so a refusal emitted as COULD NOT VERIFY was invisible to it' — dated 2026-09-18, so the line moved TODAY and this item asks where it now sits. THIS ITEM CORRECTS A PREMISE I ACTED ON: I left refusals.py out of lc-205's built write-set citing lc-33's evidence that a grep for 'COULD NOT VERIFY [' returns ZERO across the core package. That measurement was taken 2026-09-13 and is now false — it returns 3. The decision may still be right; the basis I gave for it was stale, and lc-205's new could-not-verify site is itself one of the 193. ## lc-217
grade: READY
requirement: A COMPLETED CARRIER MIGRATION THAT LEAVES ITS SOURCE BEHIND IS AN UNFINISHED MIGRATION, AND NOTHING SAYS SO. Operator ruling 2026-09-18, quoted first-hand via lifecycle-6f: 'wehn you migrate dont leave legacy fiels lying around thast someting thast important. avfter migration only the new satte shdlu exists. for legacy evergting is in git anyways'. The operator's own basis is what makes it safe — git holds every prior state, so retiring the source destroys nothing and the reversible test passes at the REPO rather than only at the desk. Today retiring is OPT-IN, so the default outcome of a carrier migration is two states coexisting and no mechanism anywhere calls that wrong. THE FIX IS POLARITY, NOT FORCE: this gates the SILENCE, never the ACT.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_migrate_residue.py
done-criterion: A completed carrier migration whose SOURCE FILE STILL EXISTS reports a FINDING naming the source path and the exit it has not taken. MUST-NOT-BUILD, and it is the heart of the ruling: this is NOT delete-by-default. The four existing preconditions on --retire-source stay EXACTLY as they are and none is softened — retire_source_uncommitted, retire_source_laws_absent, retire_source_unpinned_anchor, retire_source_not_writing — because they gate the irreversible ACT and deleting past them would be a destructive step sized to intent rather than to the object's current state. What changes is that the tool stops being silent about a migration that stopped half way: an operator who genuinely wants both states keeps them and now has to say so. RED-FIRST: a completed migration leaving BACKLOG.md in place currently exits CLEAN and must report the finding; a migration whose source was retired stays CLEAN; and the CONTROL that bounds it — a run BLOCKED by any of the four preconditions must report THAT precondition, not this new finding, since telling an operator their migration is unfinished when the tool itself refused to finish it would be a guard firing on legitimate work (law 11). THE SWEEP IS PART OF THE ITEM: already-migrated declared repos are checked for a surviving source, and each is disposed or explicitly recorded as deliberate.
evidence: RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, operator words quoted verbatim above and carried as the operator's, not paraphrased. MEASURED at this desk: the four preconditions are real and named — retire_source_uncommitted, retire_source_laws_absent, retire_source_unpinned_anchor and retire_source_not_writing all appear in the roster's own listing of arrangements, and all four sit in the set prove-rows reports as carrying NO recorded mutation, which is worth knowing before anything near them is changed. MEASURED: this repo is its own first case and has ALREADY taken the exit correctly — BACKLOG.md was deleted on migration with its legacy citations pinned to the deleting commit's parent (5c257ec), which CLAUDE.md records with a measured verification that all 16 citations resolve at exactly the cited start line. So the ruling makes general a pattern this repo already demonstrates rather than inventing one. DERIVED: the carrier-migration path is where a source genuinely survives, because the schema path reads no source at all — measured separately under lc-168.
blocked-by: NONE

## lc-220
grade: READY
requirement: THE RETIREMENT TRIPWIRE COMPUTES A LIFETIME RATIO WHERE THE RULE SPECIFIES A STRETCH, SO THE ALARM GETS QUIETER THE LONGER A REPO RUNS. The corpus rule says, in its own words, a rough tripwire of 3:1 over a plus-30-percent-LINE STRETCH. A stretch, not a lifetime. item ratio states its own window in its own output — capture added since the carrier was CREATED — and offers no window flag at all. NOT AN ARITHMETIC BUG: the sum is right. It is the ANCHOR. A denominator that only accumulates means every day of healthy draining makes the next capture-heavy day harder to detect, and a repo with enough history can eventually never trip it. That is a check going stale toward PASSING — the silent direction, where nobody looks because nothing goes red — and it is the same class as the historical-finding-count test repaired earlier today, one level up.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: The ratio is computed over a WINDOW that can go red on a capture-heavy stretch in a long-lived repo, and the verb states which window it used in its own output as it does today. THE WINDOW ITSELF IS THE DECISION AND IS NOT SETTLED HERE — do not invent a rolling window to make the number move. FIRST STEP, and it may be the real subject: since-the-last-retirement-pass requires a pass to have been RECORDED, and MEASURED HERE nothing records one, so that window is keyed to an event nothing writes — the observer gap, and choosing it without building the recording act would ship a window that never advances. RED-FIRST: a carrier whose lifetime ratio is below the tripwire while its recent stretch is above it must go RED — which is exactly this repo at this commit, so the arrangement is the repo itself and needs no fixture. CONTROL that must stay green: a carrier draining steadily over the same window stays CLEAN, since the trigger reads flow and never size.
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK, and the pair is the whole finding, same carrier same moment: item ratio reports 211:105 = 2.01:1 CLEAN over its lifetime window, while TODAY over the same carrier is 63 captured against 18 drained = 3.50:1, ABOVE the 3:1 tripwire. Both numbers are true; the rule window would fire and the tool window does not. Reproducible from git: baseline is the parent of the first ITEMS.md commit today, capture is added id headings, drain is id headings added to ITEMS-DONE.md. MY OWN FIRST COUNT WAS WRONG AND THE METHOD MATTERS, so it is recorded: a cumulative diff over the window returned 47, because an item added AND closed inside the window nets out of ITEMS.md entirely. Summing per commit returns 63. A net diff UNDER-COUNTS capture, which is the conservative direction and still wrong. RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, who found this and whose 63:18 I reproduced independently rather than carrying. MEASURED: item ratio --help offers no window flag, and grep over LEDGER.md returns ZERO mentions of a retirement pass while verbs.py only WARNS that one is owed — so nothing anywhere records a pass having happened.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 Carrying the under-count finding into the requirement at the judgment desk request, rather than leaving it in the evidence slot as a note about my method. It is not only how I mis-measured: it is a SECOND mechanism pushing the same instrument quiet, independent of the lifetime window, and two under-counts pointing the same way in one alarm is a property of the instrument rather than an anecdote about the measurer.
amended-requirement: 2026-09-18 THE RETIREMENT TRIPWIRE COMPUTES A LIFETIME RATIO WHERE THE RULE SPECIFIES A STRETCH, SO THE ALARM GETS QUIETER THE LONGER A REPO RUNS. The corpus rule says, in its own words, a rough tripwire of 3:1 over a plus-30-percent-LINE STRETCH. A stretch, not a lifetime. item ratio states its own window in its own output — capture added since the carrier was CREATED — and offers no window flag at all. NOT AN ARITHMETIC BUG: the sum is right. It is the ANCHOR. A denominator that only accumulates means every day of healthy draining makes the next capture-heavy day harder to detect, and a repo with enough history can eventually never trip it. That is a check going stale toward PASSING — the silent direction, where nobody looks because nothing goes red. TWO INDEPENDENT UNDER-COUNTING MECHANISMS POINT THE SAME WAY IN ONE INSTRUMENT, and that is the finding beyond the arithmetic: the lifetime window quiets the alarm as history accumulates, AND the obvious way to measure a window — a cumulative diff across it — NETS OUT every item added and closed inside that window, because such an item leaves the carrier entirely and never appears as an addition. Measured: 47 by cumulative diff against 63 summed per commit, same window. Both errors are CONSERVATIVE, both make capture look smaller than it was, and an alarm cannot be made safe by erring quiet — quiet is the direction that fails silently. Same class as the historical-finding-count test repaired earlier today, one level up.

## lc-221
grade: READY
requirement: THE TWO ROWS ADDED 2026-09-18 HAVE NO RE-RUNNABLE ARRANGEMENT IN prove-rows, SO THEIR RED-FIRST PROOFS DO NOT SURVIVE THE SESSION THAT RAN THEM. migration_readback_disagrees and trigger_verb_unknown were both proven red-first BY HAND and both proofs were sound — that is not what is wrong. What is wrong is that a hand proof happened ONCE, in message traffic, and the next session opening this repo sees 112 rows 112 passed with the two newest unproven and no trace of the proofs. The repo own rule names it: the hand-derivation is the prototype and the MECHANISM is the deliverable, and a check counts only once it has gone red in a way anyone can re-run. AND THE GENERAL HALF, which is the more useful one: A prove-rows RUN FILTERED TO FAILURES CANNOT SEE A ROW THAT WAS NEVER PROVEN. A row with no arrangement emits no verdict line at all, so it is absent from any filter over verdicts — rows going DARK and rows never LIT are different questions, and only the second grows silently as the roster grows. THE ROSTER-GROWTH CHECK IS arrangements AGAINST rows, NEVER A SCAN OF VERDICTS.
goal: every-refusal-red-first
write-set: tools/prove-rows.py
done-criterion: Both rows carry a recorded arrangement in prove-rows, each ADMITTED ON A PAIR per lc-142 and never on a bare PROVEN: run at the real anchor, which must give rows changed naming that row; and the same arrangement re-pointed at an INERT anchor such as a comment line, which must give rows changed NONE and FAIL. PROVEN is also what an arrangement prints when it could never have gone red, so the inert re-point is what separates a working anchor from one the mutation cannot reach. The arrangements are WRITTEN FROM THE PROOFS THAT ALREADY FIRED rather than invented — the evidence slot carries both. MUST-NOT-BUILD: no arrangement is recorded for a row whose mutation has not been run; a table entry nobody fired is the same unproven claim one layer out, which is the defect this item is about.
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK from the source of truth, and this count is the finding own reproducible witness: arrangements recorded 92, rows registered 112, rows with NO arrangement 20 — and the two newest are in that 20. Confirmed with a working control: grep over tools/prove-rows.py returns 0 for migration_readback_disagrees and 0 for trigger_verb_unknown while returning 1 for kind_grew_without_exit, so the zeros are readings and not a dead pattern. The 92 is unchanged while the roster grew 110 to 112, which is the shape stated as the general half. RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, who found it and read the no-mutation list rather than inferring it from the arithmetic; reproduced here independently. THE HAND PROOFS THE ARRANGEMENTS ARE TO BE WRITTEN FROM, both run 2026-09-18 and both recorded in their commits: migration_readback_disagrees went red by reintroducing lc-205 WHOLE into the writer — the old search with no guard — over a head it cannot match, the run printing its written: line while the read-back named the one bad target and left the other three clean, exit 2, APPLIED not claimed, migrate.py restored byte-identical; trigger_verb_unknown fires on a misspelled command path with a control that is the same kind at the same stage correctly spelled, so the verb existence is what separates them. NOT MEASURED: whether the other 18 unproven rows predate tonight — they are listed by the tool at the end of every run, never omitted, which is the roster saying how much of itself is proven and is the design working rather than a defect.

DERIVED at that desk and carried as their reading: CAPTURE WAS NOT THE FAILURE -- the goal sat in the investigation record's GOAL slot throughout, written and persisted. The failure is that no moment CONSUMED it. Ten devices passed item-level checks while nothing ever asked whether any act advanced the goal, and the operator's repetition WAS the manual execution of the missing goal-advance seam. Same human-as-mechanism shape as their pestering standing in for the round-series readout: a person performing, by hand and repeatedly, the step the environment does not fire. THIS IS THE SPECIMEN THIS ITEM MOST NEEDS because it separates the two halves the item could otherwise be read as conflating: a goal-advance slot is not a capture mechanism and would have added nothing to the writing side, which already worked; it is a CONSUMING seam, and its absence is invisible precisely because the record looks complete. CROSS-NOTE for lc-228 and M5's mining half, recorded as input and not decision: goal-restatements per arc are COUNTABLE in transcripts and are a direct measure of how often the human had to be the mechanism -- a candidate metric for the mining half rather than a decided one. NOT MEASURED HERE: this desk has not counted restatements in any transcript, and the countability claim is the driving desk's.

(a) INTERVENTION-TAXONOMY HYPOTHESES: the mining half registers its candidate classes BEFORE the pass runs, so the taxonomy is graded against a prediction instead of read off whatever the sample happens to contain, since a taxonomy fitted after the fact explains every sample and predicts none. (b) DERIVABLE-FRACTION METRIC: each mined operator question is graded on whether its answer was ALREADY DERIVABLE from what the repo held at the moment it was asked. That split decides what to build, because it separates the two weak links the design of record names: an answer NOT in the record is a CAPTURE gap, one that WAS there is a RETRIEVAL gap, and one pipeline would otherwise report them as a single number.

Independent-observer verification is the research doc strongest addition and the desk role already IS this, undocumented as a mechanism. State it as a mechanism DEFINED BY WHAT IT INDEPENDENTLY READS, never by authority over the peer, because role separation over the same information is provably dominated and only an independent SIGNAL does the work. Candidate #0 admitted by operator decision 2026-09-19 (directive D3), ranked ahead of the note own five.
goal: enforce-the-invariants

done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the design decision. The shape that is already fixed: the mechanism is defined by the ARTIFACTS it independently reads and by its refusal contract, never by authority over the peer, and it must meet the reach discipline in docs/audits/2026-09-18-robustness-clean-without-looking.md, so a verdict it emits names what it examined and an examination of nothing renders could-not-verify rather than clean. MUST-NOT-BUILD: nothing that grades the peer PROSE or its own report of what it did, since a party own account of who caught something is not evidence and the judge ceiling is AUROC 0.65.

## lc-230
grade: READY
requirement: THE VERIFY VERB COLLAPSES THREE ANSWERS INTO TWO AT THE EXACT BOUNDARY IT EXISTS TO DEFEND. run_one (verify.py:87-94) maps a wrapped command's exit to three verdicts, but the mapping sends every non-zero code outside COULD_NOT_START to ran-failed, so rc=3 -- this CLI's own COULD NOT VERIFY -- is indistinguishable from rc=1. cmd_verify then prints RAN, FAILED and exits FINDING [verify_check_failed] 'ran and returned non-zero'. A wrapped tool that honestly said it could not verify is booked as verified-wrong, inside the instrument whose whole purpose is law 1. Record: queue item 2 of directive addendum 3, measured at this desk 2026-09-19.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verify.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_verify.py
done-criterion: run_one returns a FOURTH verdict for the wrapped could-not-verify code, and cmd_verify reports it apart from failures and folds it into the run's exit by exits.worst, so a registered command that could not verify makes the whole verify run COULD NOT VERIFY rather than a FINDING. RED-FIRST, the discriminating pair, and both arms are required because a change that merely moves rc=3 somewhere new proves nothing: 'exit 3' must stop sharing a verdict with 'exit 1', AND 'exit 1' must still report as a failure. MUST-NOT-MOVE: rc=127 and rc=126 stay did-not-run, and rc=0 stays ran-clean -- those two boundaries already discriminate and this change must not disturb them. The refusal row carries the firing input and is proven red first per law 2.
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-19, executed rather than read, calling run_one directly with a four-command pair: 'exit 3' -> verdict 'ran-failed' code 3; 'exit 1' -> verdict 'ran-failed' code 1; 'exit 0' -> 'ran-clean'; 'exit 127' -> 'did-not-run'. The two boundaries that work are shown working, which is what makes the collapsed one a reading and not a guess. LIVE AND REACHABLE IN THIS REPO TODAY, not hypothetical: CLAUDE.md's Verify block registers six commands, of which 'lifecycle audit' and 'tools/prove-rows.py' both exit 3. I ran the first -- 'lifecycle audit' exits 3 and prints 'lifecycle audit: COULD NOT VERIFY'. So 'lifecycle verify' run here today reports this repo's own honest could-not-verify as RAN, FAILED. RELAYED and NOT reproduced here: the driving desk cachyos-setup-b3 confirmed the same classifier reading independently, COULD_NOT_START = (126, 127). SCOPE, so this is not over-read: addendum 3 KILLED the second arm of this defect. An earlier report that the verify verb's own exit contract was wrong rested on an rc=0 that was zsh pipe-status -- tail's exit, not the verb's -- and the executing desk's own re-run refuted it. Only the classifier arm is booked here.

## lc-231
grade: PARKED
requirement: AN ARC IS THE ONE THING THIS SYSTEM IS ABOUT AND THE ONLY THING IT DOES NOT REGISTER. Every persisted thing resolves to a registered kind (invariant 1), and 26 kinds are declared; the ARC -- the multi-session unit of work that statiker and daneel maintain and that dies with its protocol run -- is not among them. The design of record names this exactly: those protocols capture in-flight and richly, and what they do NOT do is outlive their own arc, so session 11 re-derives what session 3 knew. Lifecycle's job is to be what they write into. Record: docs/directives/2026-09-19-arc-walk-synthesis.md, and docs/answerable-not-felt.md section 'What statiker and daneel already do'.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN
done-criterion: The arc's schema is DECIDED and declared, and the decision is graded against the eight synthesis requirements rather than against walk 1 alone. The eight, which the design round answers one by one: stage exits take the full trigger vocabulary (predicate, verb, AND operator-judged); stages carry conduct constraints; the narrowing's SHAPE is a per-arc slot (eliminative / palette-with-dispositions / none); a verdict-capture seam books operator taste judgments at utterance; arcs are OPTIONAL instances, never a per-project obligation; arc-vs-lane unification is checked FIRST; external-event exits get intake observers and deadlines get a dated slot with an observer, exempt by design from the time-word ban; and outward stages are MARKED, wiring the carve-out floor into structure. THE GRADING BAR, and it is why the walks are not decoration: requirements 4, 6 and 7 are ones walk 1 alone would have got WRONG rather than merely missed, so a design justified only by the diagnosis-shaped walk fails this criterion even if it is internally consistent -- it would hardcode eliminative narrowing, predicate-only exits, and no verdict capture. MUST-NOT-BUILD: no schema change lands before lc-218's bump, per lc-218's own MUST-NOT-BUILD; an eighth or later stage is its own numbered bump.
evidence: THE DESIGN CORPUS IS TWO ARTIFACTS AND THIS ENTRY'S MESSAGE HISTORY IS NEITHER, stated because addendum 3 ruled exactly that: the doc is the carrier, the channel is not. (1) docs/directives/2026-09-19-arc-walk-synthesis.md -- four hypothetical walks over real specimens (convergent diagnosis, the Marvel Rivals freeze arc; divergent/creative, the wan2gp workflow search; routine maintenance, CachyOS general maintenance; external-consequence procedure, the B-Plan planning work) yielding eight schema requirements no single walk shows. (2) Its section 'Additional inputs': the live-working-state framing with its KILL TEST, and the belief vocabulary -- facts versus beliefs, bases, kill-conditions, citers, and reopen-WITH-PROPAGATION. The file's own closing line binds them: this file and those entry inputs are one design corpus and the design round reads both. DERIVED, not measured: the four walks are hypothetical by construction, so the eight requirements are a reasoned enumeration and not a measurement, and the entry rests on them as design inputs rather than as findings. MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-19: no 'arc' and no 'lane' kind exists in the declaration's 26, and no ledger entry rules on unification -- which is what makes requirement 6 a live first question rather than a formality. The claim was true of a channel, not of a carrier.

DERIVED inputs: (1) the trigger moments become observable events once arcs have identity -- an arc closing with no runbook cited, a second occurrence of a journey shape, a probe used twice, an operator question asked twice (M5's mine); the second-occurrence one is countable ONLY once arcs are a kind, which is this item's own justification. (2) the enforcement form is the required-slot pattern at arc close, runbook: cited <name> / minted <name> / declined <reason> -- presence computable, fill judgment, a silent skip impossible, declines auditable. (3) the boundary, stated so the round does not over-design: the environment fires the QUESTION and makes writing the lazy path, while whether a piece is WORTH minting stays judgment, operator as taste and never as trigger. Cross-reference M5's drain and the close ceremony (lc-97 territory) rather than duplicating either.

## lc-232
grade: READY
requirement: `item park` REWRITES THE BASE `blocked-by:` SLOT, so parking a block that already carries an `amended-blocked-by:` line DESTROYS the base line's prior content and leaves the amendment's own reason describing a change from a state the block no longer shows. This is NOT lc-112, which the verb already handles: lc-112 is park writing a value that would not GOVERN, and the verb refuses that by composing the write in memory and asking items.parse which value ends up in force. That refusal is silent here precisely BECAUSE the caller passes the effective value -- the check passes, the write proceeds, and what is lost is the base line it lands on. Two shapes satisfy this and either is acceptable -- park appends a superseding line rather than writing in place when an amendment to that slot exists, or park refuses and names `item amend` as the path -- and the entry does not choose, because law 8's requirement is that the history stay readable, not that a particular verb write it. RED-FIRST, with a real specimen rather than a plant: lc-37 at 14df96e's parent carries base `evidence <prose predicate>` plus `amended-blocked-by: 2026-09-13 evidence false # ...`; parking it with the effective value must NOT leave base and amendment byte-identical with the prose predicate gone. evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-19, and the specimen is a real entry rather than a construction: parking lc-37 with its effective blocker rewrote the base line. Before, the base read the original prose predicate ('evidence the sweep has not been run over any real carrier; which carriers are in scope (lifecycle ITEMS.md, dotfiles ITEMS.md, cache-fix ITEMS.md) is the fir...'); after, the base reads the 2026-09-13 amended value and the prose predicate exists only in git. READ OFF THE DIFF, not inferred: the deletion side of 14df96e carries that blocked-by line among five `grade: NEW` lines, which is how it was noticed at all. THE CONSEQUENCE, which is the part that matters: the block's own 2026-09-13 amend-reason says it was 'Completing the evidence-predicate repair pass of 3352097 ... Prose predicate ...', and the prose predicate it repaired is no longer at the block, so a reader reconstructing that amendment sees base and amendment byte-identical and a reason describing a no-op. DERIVED from verbs.py:1774-1804 (cmd_item_park's own docstring): the lc-112 guard asks whether the written value would GOVERN, which is a question about the EFFECTIVE value and not about the base line's contents, so a caller passing the effective value passes the guard by construction. SIBLING, read side: lc-165 is the rendering half of law 8's two-reads rule and its MUST-NOT-MOVE is that the amendment history stays readable; this is the WRITE verb breaching that same invariant at a different site. They share verbs.py and bundle at join time. BOUNDARY, so the finding is not over-read: in the instance that produced it nothing a reader NEEDS was lost -- the effective value is correct, the amendment chain resolves, and git holds the original. The defect is the class, not this instance's damage.

## lc-233
grade: READY
requirement: A DECISION BLOCKER CAN BE WRITTEN IN A FORM ITS OWN ANSWER IS REFUSED IN, which makes it unanswerable through the sanctioned path and gives the board a wait that cannot end. Three mechanisms meet: the blocker slot ACCEPTS a question containing the slot separator ' -- '; `ledger add decision` REFUSES a question containing it (FINDING [ledger_body], correctly, since it would reparse into different slots); and `ledger.decision_for` matches question to blocker by EXACT string equality. So a blocker carrying that separator has exactly one answer that would resolve it and that answer cannot be written. This is the `evidence false` softlock's sibling on the DECISION side, and structurally worse: a false predicate is at least expressible. Record: hit while promoting lc-8, queue item 5, 2026-09-19.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/ledger.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py,test/test_ledger.py
done-criterion: OUTCOME FIRST, not pre-deciding which side moves: a decision blocker cannot be written in a form whose answering ledger line would be refused. Two shapes satisfy it -- the blocker slot refuses the separator at WRITE time with the same message and the same rephrase-rather-than-escape instruction the ledger already gives, or the matcher compares normalised questions so the two spellings resolve to one -- and the first is preferred because it fails at the moment of writing rather than at the moment of answering, which may be months later and at a different desk. RED-FIRST: booking a decision blocker whose question contains ' -- ' must be refused, AND a blocker without it must still be accepted, because a change that merely refuses more proves nothing. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the ledger's own refusal keeps its current behaviour and wording; it is right and is not the defect. MUST-NOT-BUILD: no escaping, per that refusal's own reason -- an escaped spelling puts two forms of every value in the file and a reader cannot tell which they are looking at.
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-19, executed rather than reasoned: `ledger add decision` with lc-8's blocker question taken VERBATIM from classify_blocker (not retyped) returned FINDING [ledger_body] naming the separator, while ledger.decision_for (ledger.py:361) matches on `(ln.slots.get('question') or '').strip() == q`, exact equality. The two facts together are what make it unanswerable; either alone is harmless. POPULATION SWEPT at 40b610f rather than assumed, over BOTH homes via the tool's own parser and classify_blocker: exactly ONE live decision blocker carried the shape, lc-8's, and ZERO in the done home. Reported at n=1 and not inflated. DERIVED, and it is why this is a class rather than a typo: lc-8's blocker was written 2026-09-12 and the ledger refusal postdates it, so a refusal was added on the WRITE path without a sweep of the existing population it made unanswerable on the READ path. The sweep costs one query, which is the one run above. RESOLVED FOR lc-8 ONLY, and recorded so the fix is not thought already general: its question was REPHRASED in substance-preserving form, the decision line written, and the item promoted. The mechanism is untouched and the next such blocker deadlocks the same way.
blocked-by: NONE

DATA EXISTS: git holds every past roster state. grade: READY

Whole-carrier work stays with the triggered passes: the retirement ratio and the divergence sweep.

(b) Collision clusters PRINT AS MERGE QUESTIONS, so the join's collision output doubles as the consolidation worklist. RED-FIRST for (a): a candidate whose write-set names a path that does not exist must FIRE, and one whose paths all resolve must NOT — the second arm decides shippability, since a check that fires on everything is not a check. JUDGMENT SLICE, prose plus a required token: a per-candidate supersession check against recent closures and design decisions, rendered as a required line in the wave's route report — 'consolidation: N re-grounded, X merged, Y superseded' or 'nothing owed' — presence computable, fill quality judgment, per the required-slots pattern. MUST-NOT-BUILD: nothing that grades the fill's CONTENT, and no full-carrier pass — the scope boundary is the affordability argument and widening it silently is how this becomes the pass it was designed not to be. DESIGN QUESTIONS THIS ENTRY CARRIES RATHER THAN ANSWERS: the token's home (the waves verb's own output, or the route-line convention), and this item's relation to lc-173 and the test/-directory collapse.
evidence: ALL THREE BASES ARE MEASURED IN THIS REPO. (1) The join consumes write-set slots UNCHECKED: the backlog doctrine's own measured case is four existing files booked under a wrong prefix graded as sharing no file with any schedulable item, and a phantom path renders as a tidy standalone lane. (2) MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-19, and it is the sharper half: `item waves` over the current schedulable set returns ONE LANE OVER 102 ITEMS, because `test/` is a DIRECTORY entry written by 13 items and covering files named by 78 more, so the partition collapses transitively and the lane count stops discriminating. A desk reading only the lane count reads that as a correct serialization verdict. No code does this. The seventh stage validates its vocabulary, demands a reason after none, and checks verb <name> against the parser; a predicate value is never run. So a declared predicate that has NEVER RUN and one that runs quiet are indistinguishable in the declaration, which is the same two-states-under-one-word shape the design round carries as findings (a) through (h). Routed from wave B lc-224 pickup, where the entry's plan to mirror this evaluator revealed there is no evaluation path to mirror.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the round. The shape is a choice between two named repairs and the entry must not pre-empt it: EITHER the predicate mode gains a declared-exemption form that the predicate itself reads, so the alarm means a law went uncited WITHOUT a declared reason, OR the predicate mode is retired from TRIGGER_MODES until something evaluates it. MUST-NOT-MOVE, and it is the whole point of law 11 here: the predicate is NOT softened and the firing is NOT suppressed. A check firing on declared legitimate work trains the override reflex that kills the guard, and a check nothing evaluates is not a check at all.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-19 by executing, not by reading. lanes.evaluate_trigger has exactly three call sites: lanes.py:680 for lane triggers, verbs.py:902 and verbs.py:1730 for item evidence blockers. None is in declaration.py. POSITIVE CONTROL for that absence: the same grep returns those three real sites, so the zero for kind triggers is a reading and not a dead pattern. THE ONE KIND USING THE MODE, from the declaration: journal entries, whose trigger is predicate grep -q 'NO JOURNAL ENTRY SUPPORTS THIS' CLAUDE.md. RUN THROUGH THE REAL EVALUATOR by this desk: state FIRE, exit 0. CONFIRMED AT THE ARTIFACT with a negative control: the string is at CLAUDE.md:108, and a deliberately near-miss variant returns 0 hits, so the instrument discriminates. WHY THIS IS LAW 11 SHAPED RATHER THAN A STALE PREDICATE: the fire may be the INTENDED semantics, since the predicate reads as a law stands uncited so a journal entry is OWED. What makes it a false alarm is that the laws file DECLARES the uncited state legitimate for laws 13, 15 and 16, each carrying its own reason for standing uncited. So if this predicate were ever wired to its documented evaluator it would be a permanent alarm on declared legitimate work, and declaration.py's own commentary warns against exactly that, calling a false-firing predicate a lane that reads alarming forever and trains the override reflex that kills the guard. RELAYED from the driving desk cachyos-setup-b3, carried as theirs and NOT re-measured here: counting the uncited markers with grep -c returns 1 rather than 3, because law 16's marker WRAPS mid-phrase across the hard-wrapped prose, so a line-based count under-reads the very markers the predicate greps for. If the exemption form ends up counting markers it must not count them that way. NOT MEASURED AND DELIBERATELY NOT CLAIMED: nothing here establishes whether WRITER triggers should be wired at all, which is the round's question and not this entry's.
